# Laos — English for Primary Schools (MoES / RIES)

**Grade 3 (ປ.3) Student Textbook, 2021 edition — FULLY READ + CITATION CHECK PASSED 2026-10-27.** Laos's first English grade in this series: English starts at Grade 3, and this is the first book. Source copy: the public mirror the library's secured copy came from (GitHub Xaypanya/Lao-Textbook, `textbooks/primary/p3/p3_english_2021.pdf`). Working copy verified against the library record before reading: **41,839,690 bytes, 141 PDF pages — both match the record exactly (VERIFIED-TEXT)**. Printed folios run 1–134; **printed page = PDF page − 7** throughout (checked at the Introduction p.1 = PDF 8, Unit 2 p.22 = PDF 29, Unit 3 p.40 = PDF 47, and the back page).

## The book itself

"ENGLISH FOR PRIMARY SCHOOLS — TEXTBOOK — GRADE 3," Ministry of Education and Sports, Research Institute for Educational Sciences (RIES), 2021. The Lao foreword (unnumbered, PDF p.4) calls this the **revised edition** (ສະບັບປັບປຸງ): a communicative course — listening and speaking first as the base for reading and writing, vocabulary and grammar sized for beginners, topics tied to the children's daily lives, pair and group work, learner-centred, with songs, stories, games, role play and creative activities; it names the **phonics ("sounds") method as new in this revised curriculum** — children learn how sounds relate and use that to read and write words. A QR code on the foreword page points to extra teaching media. Writers: Manoly Dongvan, Vongmani Sitha, Viengkham Singvongsa, Soutta Inthavong, Phonesavanh Hongsomboun; Editor in Chief Khamphanh Phimsipasom; Managing Editor Manoly Dongvan; Illustrator Chongkham Phonkeo; design and layout Chaleunsak Khamsakpasit. Back page (printed p.134): ISBN **978-9932-00317-4**; printed by Siriwattana Interprint PCL, print registration 003ວພຈ02032021, run of 182,500 copies, 18×25 cm; supported by the Australian Government and the state budget, curriculum development additionally supported by the European Union; made into an e-book by the educational-media development centre and distributed free. Printed p.133 is a Lao book-care page (from a MoES/UNICEF poster). Every interior page carries a faint "RIES" watermark.

**Structure.** An Introduction plus **7 units, 29 lessons**. Each unit is three content lessons and a "Let's practice!" review lesson (Unit 7 adds the Revision lesson). A content lesson runs one fixed shape of numbered activities across about six pages: an opener (Talk about the picture / Chant / Listen and chant / Listen and sing) → a second speaking activity (Listen and point / Ask and answer / Point and say / Speak) → some lessons a headed **Grammar** box on the first page → the phonics block: **Say the sound** (one letter-sound, taught through a two-to-three-line mini-text packed with the sound) → **Trace the letter** (stroke arrows) → **Copy the letter** (four ruled lines) → **Find the letter** (early lessons: hunt the letter in a grid, with three picture words) or **Listen/Read and point** with letter tiles (later lessons: blend CVC words) → **Listen to the story** (a six-panel picture story with speech bubbles) or a listening activity → a closer (role play, match, game, speak, order the pictures). Practice lessons run: Talk about the picture → Draw → a reading/matching activity → a blending or word-building activity → a listening activity → a game or spot-the-difference.

**The phonics spine** (one sound per content lesson, in teaching order): **s** (L1) → **a** (L2) → **t** (L3) → **i** (L5) → **p** (L6) → **n** (L7) → **c/k, written "ck"** (L9) → **e** (L10) → **h** (L11) → **d** (L13) → **m** (L14) → **r** (L15) → **o** (L17) → **g** (L18) → **u** (L19) → **l** (L21) → **f** (L22) → **b** (L23) → **j** (L25) → **z** (L26) → **w** (L27). Twenty-one sounds; all five short vowels; blending with letter tiles starts at L9 and CVC reading runs to the end. Letters q, v, x, y get no sound lesson; the whole alphabet appears once, as a song, in L16. Recurring children: Sounthone, Houmphanh, Champa, Payia (and Kham, Phone, Noy, Hong, Soumaly, Moneng in name activities).

- **Introduction — Let's learn English! (pp.1–3).** 1 Sing the song (p.1): a hello song — greetings, "How are you?", and the answers "I'm good!" / "I'm great!". 2 Listen and point (pp.2–3): seven numbered pictures of a schoolchild doing classroom actions; the words are carried by the audio, none are printed (checked on the rendered p.3).
- **Unit 1 Hello (pp.4–21).** **L1 Hello (pp.4–9):** Talk about the picture (p.4); Listen and point (p.5); **s** — sound mini-text: Sounthone walking suddenly sees a snake (p.6); picture words sun, sit, snake; Trace/Copy/Find the letter s (p.7); story **The dog's name** (pp.8–9): children greet each other and give their names — "Hello! What's your name?" / "My name is Kham" (then Phone, Noy, Hong). **L2 What's your name? (pp.10–14):** **Grammar box: "What's your name? — My name is Houmphanh" (p.10)**; Chant cycling the pattern through four children's names (p.10); Ask and answer (p.11); **a** — Champa sees an ant on her apple and cries out (p.12); words ant, apple, cat; Trace/Copy/Find a (p.13); Listen and point + Do a role play (p.14). **L3 Colours (pp.15–18):** Listen and chant (p.15) — a colour chant on the frame "The X is [colour]": apple red, sun yellow, snake green, goose white, ball blue, "and the sky is too"; Point and say (p.15); **t** — Houmphanh fishing sees ten turtles in the river (p.16); words turtle, ten, ant; Trace/Copy/Find t (p.17); Look and find + Match (p.18). **L4 Let's practice! (pp.19–21):** Talk about the picture + Draw (p.19); Match the letters and pictures + Trace and say "s a t" (p.20); Listen and choose + Play the game (p.21).
- **Unit 2 In the classroom (pp.22–39).** **L5 Classroom objects (pp.22–27):** Talk about the picture — chair, pencil labelled (p.22); Listen and point — book, desk, bag, pen (p.23): the six-word classroom set; **i** — Champa reading in the school yard is bitten by a mosquito: "Oh! It's itchy!" (p.24); words itch, in, sit; Trace/Copy/Find i (p.25); story **Houmphanh's pencil** (pp.26–27): children show new things ("Look at my new pen/book/bag"), then "Where is my new pencil?" — "It's my new pencil!". **L6 What's this? (pp.28–32):** **Grammar box: "What's this? — It's a pencil" (p.28)**; Chant cycling all six classroom objects through the pattern (p.28); Ask and answer (p.29); **p** — Payia in the kitchen peeling a papaya for her pudding (p.30); words pot, pencil, pig; Trace/Copy/Find p (p.31); Listen and point + Play the game with the same question (p.32). **L7 Numbers 1–5 (pp.33–36):** Listen and sing (p.33); the count shown as "1 pig / 2 pigs" — plural -s from the start (p.33); Point and count (p.33); **n** — Sounthone's sister Noy has a new motorbike and takes him for a ride (p.34); words nut, pen, nest; Trace/Copy/Find n (p.35); Look and count (p.36). **L8 Let's practice! (pp.37–39):** Show and talk + Draw (p.37); Match the letters and pictures + Trace and say "i p n" (p.38); Look and match (numbers to letters) + Find the differences (p.39).
- **Unit 3 My family (pp.40–57).** **L9 Sounthone's family (pp.40–45):** Talk about the picture — dad, mum, brother, sister, baby around Sounthone (p.40); Listen and point (p.41); **c and k, written as the pair "ck"** — Sounthone can't find his cat; she is under the car; he calls "Kitty, Kitty, Kitty!" (p.42); Trace/Copy the letters "ck" (p.43); Listen and point with letter tiles — c-a-p, c-a-t, k-i-ck: the book's first blending (p.43); story **Sounthone's new top** (pp.44–45): "Where is my new top?" — "I don't know. Ask your dad." (then his brother; the hunt runs through the family). **L10 This is my… (pp.46–50):** **Grammar box: "This is my family" (p.46)**; Chant — a child names herself, then presents dad, mum, brother, sister: "This is my family" (p.46); Draw + Show and talk ("This is my mum," p.47); **e** — Champa's dad cooking eggs for the children (p.48); Trace/Copy e (p.49); Listen and point with tiles p-e-n, t-e-n, a-n-t (p.49); Listen and match + Speak ("This is my pencil," p.50). **L11 Feelings (pp.51–54):** Listen and sing (p.51) — six numbered pictures of feelings/body states (a child scratching, a child in the heat, a sleeping baby, a shivering child, a fanning child, a happy group); **no feeling words are printed — the set lives in the song's audio only** (verified on the rendered page; settled by the teacher guide below: the set is five words — happy, sad, hot, cold, tired — guide pp.82–84); **h** — Houmphanh walking home from school; it is very hot (p.52); Trace/Copy h (p.53); Listen and point with tiles h-a-t, h-e-n, t-a-p (p.53); Look and find — speech bubbles holding only "?" (p.54). **L12 Let's practice! (pp.55–57):** Talk about the picture + Draw (p.55); Say and point — six tile blends: p-i-n, c-a-t, t-e-n, s-i-t, a-n-t, h-a-t (p.56); Listen and match (p.57).
- **Unit 4 Animals (pp.58–75).** **L13 Animals (pp.58–63):** Talk about the picture — dog, pig, cat (p.58); Listen and point — duck, chicken, cow (p.59): the six-animal farm set; **d** — Hmong New Year: Payia dancing in the village, her dad playing a drum (p.60); Trace/Copy d (p.61); Read and point (p.61); story **The confused chick** (pp.62–63), told in summary: a lost chick asks animal after animal and is told "You're not a pig!" / "You're not a cow!" / "You're not a duck!" until the hen's clucking tells it the truth — "Oh! I'm a chicken!". **L14 Is it a…? (pp.64–68):** **Grammar box: "Is it a pig? — Yes, it is. / Is it a cow? — No, it isn't" (p.64)**; Chant on the pattern (p.64); Ask and answer (p.65); **m** — Payia and her mum sitting on a mat eating a mango: "Mmm" (p.66); Trace/Copy m (p.67); Read and point (p.67); Listen and answer + Speak (p.68). **L15 In, on, under (pp.69–72):** Listen and sing (p.69) — a six-line farm song, each line an animal, a place word and the animal's sound: cat on the roof (Meow), duck in the pond (Quack), cow under the tree (Moo), chicken on the rock (Cluck), pig in the mud, dog under the tractor; **r** — Grandma's dog sees a rat sitting on a rock and growls (p.70); Trace/Copy r (p.71); Read and point (p.71); Listen and point twice, the second set naming in/on/under (p.72). **L16 Let's practice! (pp.73–75):** Listen and sing — **the alphabet song, all 26 letters, lowercase (p.73)**; Draw (p.73); Read and point — the -at family built by swapping the first letter: hat, rat, cat, mat (p.74); What is the first sound? (p.74); Complete the words from a letter bank (d c r t i): ca_, _uck, p_g, _ow, _at (p.75).
- **Unit 5 Food (pp.76–93).** **L17 Food (pp.76–81):** Talk about the picture — soup, rice, mango (p.76); Listen and point — bread, meat, fish (p.77): the six-food set; **o** — Sounthone is hot and turns the fan on; his sister is cold and turns it off (p.78; hot/cold and on/off ride in the sound text); Trace/Copy o (p.79); Read and point — on, hot, hop, rock (p.79); story **The birthday cakes** (pp.80–81): children count their cakes — "I have two cakes!" / three / four. **L18 I like… / I don't like… (pp.82–86):** **Grammar box: "I like bread. / I don't like mango" (p.82)**; Chant cycling like/don't like through all six foods (p.82); Speak (p.83; cake appears here as a liked food); **g** — a green gecko on the wall sees Champa and runs away (p.84); Trace/Copy g (p.85); Read and point — dog, pig, egg, peg (p.85); Listen and point + Ask and answer on **"Do you like…? — Yes, I do. / No, I don't" (p.86)**. **L19 Numbers 6–10 (pp.87–90):** Listen and count — six apples, seven mangoes, eight bananas, nine oranges, ten papayas (p.87); Count and say — five picture addition sums to complete (p.87); **u** — Payia walking to the market; rain starts; she puts up her umbrella (p.88); Trace/Copy u (p.89); Read and point — up, mum, sun, cup (p.89); Look and match (p.90). **L20 Let's practice! (pp.91–93):** Talk about the picture + Draw (p.91); Read (a letter grid) + Make the words (ant, dog, pen, hand among the targets, p.92); Look and say + Look and count with written "= ___" answers (p.93).
- **Unit 6 Let's play (pp.94–111).** **L21 Having fun (pp.94–99):** Talk about the picture — jump, fly, run, swim (p.94); Listen and point — sing, hop (p.95): the six-action verb set; **l** — Houmphanh gives his cat milk; she likes it, drinks it and licks her lips (p.96); Trace/Copy l (p.97); Read and point — leg, lip, log, lock (p.97); story **Houmphanh and the bird** (pp.98–99): a **wordless** six-panel story — from the pictures: a boy finds a fallen bird, cares for it with a friend, and sets it free (no speech is printed anywhere in the story; checked on the rendered p.99; its spoken text is audio Track 6.4, printed in the teacher guide below, p.151). **L22 I can… / I can't… (pp.100–104):** **Grammar box: "I can jump. / I can't fly" (p.100)**; Chant of can/can't pairs (jump/hop, sing/fly, run/swim, p.100); Speak (p.101); **f** — the family fishing at the river; Sounthone catches five fish; it is fun (p.102); Trace/Copy f (p.103); Read and point — fan, fat, off, fun (p.103); Listen and point + Ask and answer on **"Can you…? — Yes, I can. / No, I can't" (p.104)**. **L23 Toys (pp.105–108):** Listen and chant — a guessing chant, "What's in the toy box?": "Is it a ball/car/marble/bike/doll?" — "No, it isn't" until the doll's "Yes, it is!" (p.105); Point and say (p.105); **b** — the boys have a big blue ball and bounce it with their hands (p.106); Trace/Copy b (p.107); Read and point — bag, big, bat, bin (p.107); Order the pictures (p.108). **L24 Let's practice! (pp.109–111):** Talk about the picture + Draw (p.109); Read and match — rhyming pairs dad–sad, hot–pot, fun–run, can–man (p.110); Make the words from a letter bank (p.110); Look and say ("I can run. / I can't swim") + Play the game (p.111).
- **Unit 7 My home (pp.112–132).** **L25 My house (pp.112–117):** Talk about the picture — TV, door, sofa (p.112); Listen and point — table, bed, window (p.113): the six-word home set; **j** — Sounthone in his garden; a big mango tree; he is jumping to pick a mango, and the sound text carries the book's first "There is a…" sentence (p.114); Trace/Copy j (p.115); Read and point — jug, jump, jam, jog (p.115); story **Champa's new house** (pp.116–117), in summary: Champa does not want to move; she says goodbye to her bedroom, the mango tree and the gecko, then sees the new room — "Wow! I like my new bedroom." **L26 There is a… (pp.118–122):** **Grammar box: "There is a table. / There is a bed" (p.118)**; Chant — "In my house, there is a…" through all six home words (p.118); Speak (p.119); **z** — Payia picking flowers in her garden; a big bee zooming and buzzing around the flowers (p.120); Trace/Copy z (p.121); Read and point — zip, buzz, zap (p.121); Listen and point + Look and say (p.122). **L27 What colour is it? (pp.123–126):** Listen and chant — **the colour chant**: red, yellow, pink, green, orange, purple, blue, black, brown, white (p.123; pink, orange, purple, black, brown are new — the L3 set returns inside the full ten); Point and say (p.123); **w** — Houmphanh walking; a storm starts; rain falls, wind blows, he is very wet (p.124); Trace/Copy w (p.125); Read and point — wet, web, wind, swim (p.125); Order the pictures — put the six pictures of the Champa's-new-house story in order (p.126). **L28 Let's practice! (pp.127–129):** Listen and draw — the heard line is a colour + home sentence ("There is a brown table," p.127); Draw (p.127); Read and match — rhymes cat–mat, bin–win, dog–jog, pen–ten (p.128); Complete the words — d_d, _ed, ru_, s_ck (p.128); Find the differences (p.129). **L29 Revision (pp.130–132):** Point and say over ten numbered pictures (p.130); Read and point — in, ten, hat, big, sun, hot (p.131); **Write the word (p.131) — the book's only free-writing step**; Listen and point over eight numbered pictures (p.132).

**Grammar inventory (every headed Grammar box, plus patterns taught inside stories, chants and speech activities):** What's your name? / My name is… (Intro p.1 song; story pp.8–9; Grammar L2 p.10) · "The X is [colour]" (L3 chant p.15) · Look at my new… / Where is my new…? / It's my new… (L5 story pp.26–27) · What's this? / It's a… (Grammar L6 p.28) · plural -s in counting, 1 pig / 2 pigs (L7 p.33) · I don't know / Ask your… (L9 story p.44) · This is my… (Grammar L10 p.46) · You're not a…! / I'm a…! (L13 story pp.62–63) · Is it a…? / Yes, it is / No, it isn't (Grammar L14 p.64) · in / on / under (L15 song p.69) · I have + number (L17 story pp.80–81) · I like… / I don't like… (Grammar L18 p.82) · Do you like…? / Yes, I do / No, I don't (L18 p.86) · I can… / I can't… (Grammar L22 p.100) · Can you…? / Yes, I can / No, I can't (L22 p.104) · I like my house / I don't want to move (L25 story pp.116–117) · There is a… (seeded L25 p.114; Grammar L26 p.118) · What colour is it? stands as L27's lesson frame (p.123); its printed work is the colour chant and Point and say — no printed answer pattern for the question was found in the text layer.

**Songs and chants:** hello song (Intro p.1) · colour chant, five colours (p.15) · What's this? chant (p.28) · numbers 1–5 song (p.33) · family chant (p.46) · feelings song — audio only (p.51) · Is it a…? chant (p.64) · in/on/under farm song with animal sounds (p.69) · alphabet song (p.73) · likes chant (p.82) · I can chant (p.100) · toy-box guessing chant (p.105) · There is a… chant (p.118) · the colour chant, ten colours (p.123). **Stories:** The dog's name (L1) · Houmphanh's pencil (L5) · Sounthone's new top (L9) · The confused chick (L13) · The birthday cakes (L17) · Houmphanh and the bird (L21, wordless) · Champa's new house (L25) — plus the two-to-three-line sound mini-text in every content lesson. **Exercise shapes:** talk about the picture · listen and point · ask and answer · point and say · show and talk · speak · chant/sing · trace the letter · copy the letter · find the letter · blending with letter tiles (listen/read and point) · read and point · listen to the story · role play · match (letters–pictures, listening, rhyming words) · look and find · find the differences · look and count · count and say (picture sums) · complete the words · make the words · order the pictures · draw / listen and draw · write the word · play the game.

## What looks new for the LiqLearns English ladder (from this book)

- **A complete sound-first teaching order** (Level 2–3 material): s-a-t-i-p-n first, then ck, e, h, d, m, r, o, g, u, l, f, b, j, z, w — the child can read real CVC words from the third lesson on; the alphabet song arrives only at L16 (p.73), as a review after sixteen sounds are already working. A full worked example of "sounds before alphabet names."
- **Blending by letter tiles** from L9 (c-a-p / c-a-t / k-i-ck, p.43) and the **-at family built by swapping the first letter** (hat/rat/cat/mat, p.74) plus "What is the first sound?" (p.74) — first-sound and word-family steps for the Sounds Together / First Words levels.
- **Feelings taught with no printed words** (L11, p.51): the six-picture song is the feelings set; the words around it in the book are "It's itchy!" (p.24), hot (L11's sound text p.52; L17 p.78) and cold (p.78) — a feelings lesson whose Laos evidence is picture-and-audio first, matching the platform's pictures-and-sound first stage.
- **Position words inside a song**: in/on/under taught as six sung farm lines with animal sounds (p.69) — a ready-made media-slot shape for the in/on/under lesson.
- **Numbers 6–10 through fruit and first sums**: counting six-to-ten fruits, then five picture addition sums on the same page (p.87) — numbers and adding meet in one lesson.
- **Do you like…? with Yes, I do / No, I don't** (p.86) and **Can you…?** (p.104): yes/no questions with short answers, each grown out of the statement lesson before it — Laos's Grade 3 evidence for the Questions level.
- **A wordless story for retelling** (L21, pp.98–99) and **story-ordering as the check** (L27, p.126, re-ordering L25's story): two story-level shapes not in the built lessons yet.
- **Write the word** (Revision, p.131): the single step in the whole book where the child writes a word freely — everything before it is tracing and copying; a clean marker of where this system puts first writing.
- Laos-flavoured stock for examples: Hmong New Year dancing and a drum (p.60), a gecko on the wall (pp.84, 117), papaya/papayas and mango trees, a farm of pond, mud and tractor (p.69), and the name set Sounthone, Houmphanh, Champa, Payia, Kham, Phone, Noy, Hong, Soumaly, Moneng.

**Slips recorded as printed:** "Googbye, gecko." for "Goodbye" in the L25 story (p.117 — seen on the rendered page; the teacher guide prints "Goodbye, gecko" in full, p.176, so the slip is the student book's own) · in the L15 song (p.69) the pig's sound is printed "Oud! Oud! Oud!" and the dog's "Hong! Hong! Hong!" where the other four animals get the English sounds (Meow, Quack, Moo, Cluck) — Lao animal sounds carried into the English song, as printed · the c/k lesson (L9) teaches, traces and copies the two letters as one pair, "ck" (pp.42–43) · a file curiosity, not print: this tagged PDF's accessibility layer carries picture descriptions that are not visible on the page (e.g. "Picture of a person who is very wet," p.125; a six-line description of the story pictures, p.126, one of them ungrammatical — "looking happy sat on her bed"); the printed p.125 itself shows only wet/web/wind/swim, checked on the render.

**FULLY READ 2026-10-27.** Read page by page from the text layer (the book has a real text layer; the five picture-carried points — the Introduction's action pictures, the feelings set, the wordless bird story, the "Oud" line, "Googbye," and the p.125 read-and-point words — were checked on rendered pages). **CITATION CHECK PASSED 2026-10-27** — anchors re-opened and confirmed on their pages; the filing was then spot-verified independently before commit (Grammar p.10, the in/on/under song's "Oud!" line p.69, the alphabet song p.73, the numbers page p.87, "Googbye, gecko" p.117, the ISBN on the back page, and the printed=PDF−7 offset on the rendered story page 44): hello song "I'm good! ## The book itself

"ENGLISH FOR PRIMARY SCHOOLS — TEACHER GUIDE — GRADE 3," Ministry of Education and Sports, Research Institute for Educational Sciences (RIES), 2021. Back page (printed p.202): ISBN **978-9932-00320-4** (the student book is 978-9932-00317-4); printed by Theppanya Printing Company Sole Co., Ltd., print registration 006ວພຈ02032021, run of **13,000 copies**, 21×29.7 cm (A4 — the student book is a smaller 18×25 cm); supported by the Australian Government and the state budget, curriculum development additionally supported by the European Union; made into an e-book by the educational-media development centre and distributed free. Title page (PDF p.2): writers Vongmani Sitha, Viengkham Singvongsa, Soutta Inthavong, Phonesavanh Hongsomboun, Manoly Dongvan, Chongkham Phonkeo; Managing Editor Khamphanh Phimsipasom; Editor in Chief Viengkham Singvongsa; design and layout Chaleunsak Khamsakpasit — the same team as the student book, with the two editor roles swapped between the two books as printed. PDF p.3 is a Lao reviewers/acknowledgements page.

**What kind of book it is.** A bilingual (Lao–English) script for teaching the student book: every student-book lesson gets one full lesson plan, and the guide prints everything the student book leaves to the audio — every song, chant, mini-text, story script and listening script, with answer keys. Lesson numbering, activity numbering and page order line up with the student book one-to-one, so anything cited here by lesson matches the student-book filing's lesson map.

## The method pages (Introduction, printed pp.5–7) — what the guide adds before any lesson

- **Materials the teacher makes (p.5).** **Phonics cards** for every sound taught in Grade 3, the letters drawn exactly as printed in the student book (a fan of the s-a-t-i-p-n cards is pictured). **Word cards** with simple CV/CVC words, used from Part 4 (Unit 4) onward and posted around the classroom; the pictured example set is bag, book, pencil, pen, desk, chair.
- **Assessment (section 5, pp.5–6).** Each unit's four lessons each assess a different skill: **Lesson 1 — vocabulary and listening; Lesson 2 — grammar and speaking** (speaking assessed in the pair activity, hour 3, practice stage); **Lesson 3 — writing** (copying the unit's three new letters, assessed in hour 2 or from notebooks collected after the hour; letter writing is also watched across hours 1–3); **Lesson 4 — reading** (sound links from Units 1–4 first, later reading words from Units 5–7; assessed in hour 2, practice stage). Every lesson plan carries a 0–3 rubric for its assessed skill, and rubric marks convert to /10 (worked table on p.6: rubric 3 → 10, 2 → 7, 1 → 3, 0 → 0). So no child is marked on everything every week, the class is split into **Group 1 and Group 2 (10–15 pupils each)** and the groups alternate which lessons they are assessed on, by a printed 7-unit rotation table (p.6).
- **The week and the unit (section 6, pp.6–7).** English runs **one lesson per week, each lesson 3 hours**; a unit is 4 lessons = 12 hours, and every unit has the same shape (table, p.7): Lesson 1 — hour 1 vocabulary set 1, hour 2 sounds & letters (sound 1), hour 3 the story, part 1; Lesson 2 — hour 1 grammar part 1, hour 2 sounds & letters (sound 2), hour 3 grammar part 2; Lesson 3 — hour 1 vocabulary set 2, hour 2 sounds & letters (sound 3), hour 3 the story, part 2; Lesson 4 — hour 1 creative activity, hour 2 review of sounds & letters, hour 3 review lesson.
- **The lesson-plan shape** (used for all 29 lessons + the Introduction lesson): I. Objectives · II. Assessment (the 0–3 rubric) · III. Main content · IV. Teaching media (student book, the named Audio Tracks, real objects where used) · V. Teaching activities by hour, each hour in stages — lead-in ≈5 min, main activity ≈15–25 min, practice & use ≈15–25 min, summary ≈5 min — with an "Assessment" check note after activities. Every plan is headed "time: 3 hours."

## The Introduction lesson — "Let's learn English!" (printed pp.8–11)

The guide prints the three things the student book carries only in pictures and audio:

- **The seven classroom commands** (p.8): **Stand up, Sit down, Open your book, Point to…, Look, Listen, Say** — each with its Lao gloss. No assessment in the first week.
- **The hello song, Audio Track 0.1 — full words (p.8)**: a Hello / How are you? call-and-response whose answer lines are "I'm good!" and "I'm great!" (each sung twice). Actions (p.9): Hello = wave; How are you? = point at the pupils; I'm good! = thumbs up; I'm great! = raise both arms high. Taught line by line, the class split in halves singing alternate lines, then in groups.
- **The game "Soutta Says"** (pp.9–11): the Simon-Says game under this printed name — act only when the command is prefaced with "Soutta says"; act without it and you are out and sit down; play to a winner. The game returns in Lesson 21 (p.147) and in the Revision lesson (p.198).
- **Audio Track 0.2 (Listen and point) — script and answer key (p.11)**: heard order 1. stand up, 2. look, 3. point to, 4. say, 5. sit down, 6. open your book, 7. listen; the printed key gives the student book's picture numbers in heard order: **3, 4, 1, 6, 5, 7, 2**.

## What the guide adds, lesson by lesson

For every lesson: the assessed skill and rubric, the full audio scripts, the answer keys, and the guide-only games. The phonics additions (action + formation rhyme + mini-text for every sound) are collected in one list after the units.

### Unit 1 Hello

- **L1 Hello (pp.12–18).** Assessed: vocabulary + listening; rubric counts the four characters identified (Champa, Houmphanh, Payia, Sounthone). Track 1.1 heard order: Payia, Champa, Houmphanh, Sounthone. Sound **s** mini-text (Track 1.2, p.14): Sounthone walking suddenly sees a snake and hears its hiss. Track 1.4 clap-when-it-starts-with-s list: Sounthone, sit down, look, Payia, snake, stand up, listen, book, say. **Story script, Track 1.5 "The dog's name," printed in full (pp.16–17)**, English with Lao: walking home from school, the four children meet an old lady (Payia asks her name — Kham), a boy (Houmphanh asks — Phone), a girl (Champa asks — Noy), then a dog; Sounthone asks the dog its name and it barks "Hong!"; everyone laughs at Sounthone. Thumbs-up/down check statements with answers (p.18): e.g. "The girl's name is Kham" is wrong (she is Noy); "Sounthone talks to a cat" is wrong (a dog).
- **L2 What's your name? (pp.19–25).** Grammar chant Tracks 1.6/1.7 printed in full (p.20): "What's your name? My name is Champa / Sounthone / Payia / Houmphanh," 1.7 the echo version with Lao glosses. Sound **a** mini-text (Track 1.8, p.21): Champa sees an ant on her apple and cries "Aaaaah!" Track 1.10 clap list: ant, apple, Champa, cat, Sounthone, look, hello. Track 1.11 dialogue scripts (p.24); printed matching answers (p.25): dialogue a → picture 3, b → picture 2, c → picture 1, d → picture 4.
- **L3 Colours (pp.26–32).** **Colour chant, Track 1.12, printed in full (p.27)**: "The apple is red / The sun is yellow / The snake is green / The goose is white / The ball is blue / And the sky is too" (the sun line repeats inside the chant as printed). Sound **t** mini-text (Track 1.13, pp.28–29): Houmphanh fishing at the river sees ten turtles. Track 1.15 clap list: turtle, ten, Houmphanh, red, ant, white, blue. Extra game (p.28): **colour corners** — the room's corners are named by colour; run to the corner the teacher calls; wrong corner, sit down. Story match answers (p.32): Payia–old lady, Houmphanh–boy, Champa–girl, Sounthone–dog.
- **L4 Let's practice! (pp.34–39).** Creative lesson; sounds s, a, t; assessed: reading. Match-the-letters answers (p.37): s → pictures 1 and 5; a → 3 and 6; t → 2 and 4. Track 1.16 Listen and choose — script and answers (p.38): 1. yellow, 2. white, 3. green, 4. red, 5. yellow, 6. blue. Extra group game **"What's missing?"** (p.39): each group holds a set of red/blue/green/yellow/white objects; all but the leader close their eyes; the leader removes one object and asks "What's missing?"; the group names the missing colour; leaders rotate.

### Unit 2 In the classroom

- **L5 Classroom objects (pp.40–46).** Media include the real objects. Track 2.1 heard order: 1. chair, 2. pencil, 3. bag, 4. desk, 5. book, 6. pen. Sound **i** mini-text (Track 2.2, p.42): Champa reading in the school yard is bitten by a mosquito — "Oh! It's itchy!" Track 2.4 clap list: ink, itch, yellow, name, listen, green, sit. **Story script, Track 2.5 "Houmphanh's pencil," printed in full (p.45; repeated p.60)**: walking to school, Houmphanh's pencil falls out of his bag unnoticed; in class Champa shows a new pen, Sounthone a new book, Payia a new bag; Houmphanh opens his bag — "Where is my new pencil?"; the teacher holds up a pencil — "Whose pencil is this?" — "It's my pencil." True/false statements for the role-play printed (p.46): e.g. "Champa has a new book" is wrong (a new pen); "Houmphanh's friends found his pencil" is wrong (the teacher did).
- **L6 What's this? (pp.47–53).** Grammar chant Tracks 2.6/2.7 printed in full (p.48), cycling "What's this? It's a…" through book, pen, bag, desk, chair, pencil. Sound **p** word set in the objectives (p.47): pen, pan, pencil, pumpkin, pig. Mini-text (Track 2.8, p.50): Payia in the kitchen peeling a papaya for her pudding. Track 2.10 clap list: Payia, book, pen, pencil, ten, red, pot. Track 2.11 prints six short dialogues (p.52) — heard order 1. pen, 2. bag, 3. desk, 4. book, 5. chair, 6. pencil — while the feedback key printed on the same page lists 1. pen, 2. bag, 3. book, 4. desk, 5. chair, 6. pencil (items 3/4 swapped between script and key; both recorded as printed). Extra game (p.53): pairs — one pupil, eyes covered or closed, feels an object the partner hands over and answers "What's this?" by naming it.
- **L7 Numbers 1–5 (pp.54–61).** **Song "Five little pigs," Track 2.12, printed in full (pp.55–56)**: counting-down verses five → one — a verse per number: the little pigs "went walking one day, over the hills and far away. Mother pig says: 'Oud! Oud! Come back!'" and one fewer comes back each verse — then none come back, the sad mother pig goes herself, and all five come back. Teacher's printed note (p.55): 1 pig takes no -s; 2 pigs takes -s (the plural point, taught inside the song). Actions: hold up the matching number of fingers. Extra game (p.56): the teacher calls a number and pupils form groups of that size; pupils left out sit down. Sound **n** mini-text (Track 2.13, p.58): Sounthone's sister Noy has a new motorbike and takes Sounthone for a ride. Track 2.15 clap list: nut, three, no, Payia, pencil, five, green. Look-and-count answers over the Lesson 5 story pictures (p.61): desks 4, books 3, pencil 1, pens 3, chairs 2, bags 5.
- **L8 Let's practice! (pp.62–67).** Creative lesson; sounds i, p, n; assessed: reading. Extra phonics work (p.64): **first-sound and last-sound questions** (pen: first sound p, last sound n) over snake, noodle, apple, ink, turtle, pencil, nest, itch, ant, sun, ten, cat, sit, pen. Match-the-letters answers (p.64): p → pictures 1 and 3; i → 2 and 6; n → 4 and 5. Look-and-match answers (p.66): 1–e, 2–b, 3–c, 4–d, 5–a. Find-the-differences answers — five differences (pp.66–67): yellow vs green pencil; two vs three books; one red pen vs a red and a blue pen; one chair vs no chair; bags under the desk and on the chair vs under the desk only.

### Unit 3 My family

- **L9 Sounthone's family (pp.68–74).** Family words mum, dad, brother, sister, baby (the guide's Lao glosses render brother as younger brother and sister as older sister). Sound **c/k ("ck")**: cat, cap, kick; oral CVC blending begins in this unit. Mini-text (Track 3.2, p.70): Sounthone cannot find his cat; the cat is under the car; he calls "Kitty, Kitty, Kitty!" Blending Listen-and-point (Track 3.4, p.72): pupils hear the segmented words c-a-t, k-i-ck, c-a-p — not whole words — and point; answers: kick → picture 2 (3 fingers), cap → picture 3 (1 finger), cat → picture 1 (2 fingers). **Story script, Track 3.5 "Sounthone's new top," printed in full (pp.73–74; repeated pp.80, 88)**: Sounthone has a new top for the festival (the English text as printed calls it the "Krer festival"; the Lao text names the Khmu New Year festival) but cannot find it; he asks Mum ("I don't know… Ask your dad"), then Dad ("I don't know… Ask your brother"), then his brother Khamsy ("I don't know"); Sounthone is very sad — then he sees his cat sitting on his new top; he is very happy and shows the top to his family. Agree/Disagree check: pupils walk to the agree or disagree side of the room per statement; five statements with answers (p.74): e.g. "Sounthone asks his sister" is not correct (he asks his brother).
- **L10 This is my… (pp.75–81).** Grammar chant Tracks 3.6/3.7 printed (pp.76–77): "My name is Champa. This is my mum / dad / brother / sister. This is my family." Syllable-clapping warm-up: Cham-pa, mum, dad, bro-ther, sis-ter, ba-by (p.76). "What's missing?" letter game on the board over s, a, t, i, p, n, c (p.77). Sound **e** mini-text (Track 3.8, p.78): Champa's dad is cooking eggs for Champa and her brother. Blending Track 3.10 (p.79): t-e-n, a-n-t, p-e-n — answers: ten → picture 2, ant → picture 3, pen → picture 1. Track 3.11 Listen and match scripts and answers (p.80): Sounthone — pencil, Champa — bag, Payia — book, Houmphanh — pen.
- **L11 Feelings (pp.82–89).** The guide settles the student book's audio-only feelings set: **five feeling words — happy, sad, hot, cold, tired** (p.82). **The happy song, Track 3.12 — full words printed (pp.83–84)**: verses on the "If you're happy and you know it…" frame, one per feeling — happy (clap your hands; a second happy verse ends "say hello. Hello!"), sad (shake your head), tired (close your eyes), cold (rub your arms), hot (fan your face) — each verse in a long form and a short repeated form, with a full Lao translation (p.84). Feeling actions printed with the song (p.84). Sound **h** reading text (Track 3.13, p.85): Houmphanh is walking home from school; it is very hot; his mum is happy (to see him) — with the guide's comprehension questions and expected answers in Lao. Blending Track 3.15 (p.87): h-e-n, t-a-p, h-a-t (picture 1 = hen, 2 = tap, 3 = hat). Look-and-find "Who is missing?" answers (p.89): 1. Sounthone's sister, 2. Sounthone's dad, 3. Sounthone's mum — the student book's "?" speech-bubble activity is a who-is-missing exercise over the story pictures.
- **L12 Let's practice! (pp.90–94).** Creative lesson; sounds c, e, h; assessed: reading. Track 3.16 Listen and match script (p.93): 1. Sounthone feels happy, 2. The baby feels tired, 3. Dad feels happy, 4. Mum feels cold, 5. Brother feels sad, 6. Sister feels hot; the picture key (p.94): 1. happy, 2. tired, 3. sad, 4. cold, 5. happy, 6. hot. Extra games: mime-a-feeling pairs (one mimes, the other names the feeling, p.94) and a **letter-tap team race** (p.92): five teams; the teacher says a word (e.g. pin) and asks what sound it starts with; team runners race to tap the right letter on the board; first correct tap scores.

### Unit 4 Animals

- **L13 Animals (pp.95–100).** Animals cow, cat, dog, duck, chicken, pig. Track 4.1 heard order: 1. cat, 2. dog, 3. pig, 4. duck, 5. chicken, 6. cow. Sound **d** mini-text (Track 4.2, p.97): "It's Hmong New Year. Payia is dancing in the village. Her dad is playing a drum." **Story script, Track 4.4 "The confused chick," printed in full (p.99; Lao p.100)**: a chick hatches from his egg, all alone; he sees a pig rolling in the mud — "Am I a pig?" — "Oud! Oud! You're not a pig"; a cow eating grass — "Am I a cow?" — "Moo! Moo! You're not a cow"; a duck swimming in the pond — "Am I a duck?" — "Quack! Quack! You're not a duck"; the chick feels sad; suddenly he hears "Cluck! Cluck! Cluck!" and sees his mum, brothers and sisters — "Oh I'm a chicken." True/False statements with answers (p.100): the first animal seen was the duck = false (the pig); the cow was rolling in the mud = false (eating grass); the chick was alone when he hatched = true; the duck was swimming = true; the chick has brothers and sisters = true. Role-play: the class in four animal groups.
- **L14 Is it a…? (pp.101–107).** Assessed: speaking. Grammar chant Tracks 4.5/4.6 printed in full (pp.102–103): an animal sound plays, a voice guesses "Is it a…?" until the right animal answers "Yes, it is" — the sound set as printed: chicken Cluck, cat Meow, duck Quack, dog Hong, pig Oud, cow Moo; Track 4.6 is the echo version with pauses. Sound **m** mini-text (Track 4.7, p.104): "Payia and her mum are sitting on a mat eating a mango. 'Mmm,' says Payia." Track 4.9 Listen and answer script (p.106): 1. Is it a pen? 2. Is it a chicken? 3. Is it a cow? 4. Is it a dog? 5. Is it a book? 6. Is it a pig? — printed answers (p.107): Yes, No, Yes, Yes, No, Yes (keyed to the student book's six mixed pictures).
- **L15 in, on, under (pp.108–114).** Assessed: writing. The prepositions are taught by demonstration before the song: the teacher puts a pen on/in/under a book and the class says "The pen is on the book" and so on (p.108). **The Animal Song, Track 4.10 — printed in full (p.109)**: "The cat is on the roof. Meow! Meow! Meow! / The duck is in the pond. Quack! Quack! Quack! / The cow is under the tree. Moo! Moo! Moo! / The chicken is on the rock. Cluck! Cluck! Cluck! / The pig is in the mud. Oud! Oud! Oud! / The dog is under the tractor. Hong! Hong! Hong!" Sound **r** mini-text (Track 4.11, p.111): "Grandma's dog sees a rat. The rat is sitting on a rock. The dog says, 'Rrrrr'." Track 4.13 word key (p.114): a cow → picture 2, a chicken → 3, an egg → 1, a chick → 5, a duck → 4, a pig → 6. Track 4.14 sentences: a. The chick is under a tree; b. The chick is in a box; c. The chick is on a rock — printed picture key: a → 3, b → 1, c → 2. Hour 3 re-reads the chick story (Track 4.4) with discussion questions, including "What does the cow/pig/duck say to the chick?"
- **L16 Let's practice! (pp.115–119).** Creative lesson; sounds d, r, m; assessed: reading. **The Alphabet Song, Track 4.15 — printed in full (p.116)**: the letter names a–z in order, closing "Now I know my abc, won't you sing along with me?" The guide's teaching note makes the design explicit: the pupils have learned letter **sounds** so far; this song teaches the letter **names**, which are different from the sounds. First-sound answers (p.118): 1. p (pig), 2. d, 3. c. Complete-the-words answers (p.119): 1. cat (missing t), 2. duck, 3. pig, 4. cow, 5. rat. Extra games: the letter-tap team race again, and a **group blending race** — the teacher says a word in sounds (s-a-d), groups write the word, the first correct group scores; played over it, red, ant, pen, man, sit.

### Unit 5 Food

- **L17 Food (pp.120–126).** Foods rice, meat, fish, soup, bread, mango. Track 5.1 heard order: 1. soup, 2. mango, 3. rice, 4. bread, 5. meat, 6. fish. Sound **o** mini-text (Track 5.2, p.122): "Sounthone is hot. He turns on the fan. Sounthone's sister is cold. She turns off the fan." **Story script, Track 5.4 "The birthday cakes," printed in full (pp.124–125)**: today is Champa's birthday party and all her friends are coming; her mum makes her a big banana cake; Houmphanh brings a mango cake ("Oh! Thank you! I have two cakes"); Payia brings a chocolate cake ("I have three cakes"); Sounthone brings an apple cake ("I have four cakes"); Champa has four cakes and invites all her neighbours to the party to share them. Comprehension questions with printed answers (p.125): How many cakes? 4 — banana cake from Mum, mango cake from Houmphanh, chocolate cake from Payia; the end: she invites her neighbours to share.
- **L18 I like… / I don't like… (pp.127–132).** Assessed: speaking. Grammar chant Tracks 5.5/5.6 printed (p.128): "I like mango. I like fish. I like rice. / I don't like bread. I don't like meat. I don't like soup." Sound **g** mini-text (Track 5.7, p.129): "There is a green gecko on the wall. The gecko sees Champa and it runs away." Track 5.9 Listen and point script (p.132): 1. Do you like rice? Yes, I do. 2. Do you like bread? Yes, I do. 3. Do you like mango? No, I don't. 4. Do you like cake? Yes, I do. 5. Do you like soup? No, I don't — food key in picture order: rice, bread, mango, cake, soup.
- **L19 Numbers 6–10 (pp.133–139).** Assessed: writing. **Track 5.10 "Count the fruit" printed in full (p.134)**: Six apples, Seven mangoes, Eight bananas, Nine oranges, Ten papayas — each group then counted out one by one to its number. Written answer key for the student book's count-and-say pictures (p.134): 1. 6, 2. 9, 3. 8, 4. 7, 5. 10. Sound **u** mini-text (Track 5.11, p.135): "Payia is walking to the market. It starts to rain. She puts up her umbrella." Look-and-match answers — who brought which cake (p.139): Payia → picture 1 (chocolate cake), Houmphanh → picture 2 (mango cake), Sounthone → picture 3 (apple cake), Champa's mum → picture 4 (banana cake). Extra: clap-the-number — the teacher says a number word, pupils clap that many times.
- **L20 Let's practice! (pp.140–145).** Creative lesson; sounds o, g, u; media: the Alphabet Song again (Track 4.15); assessed: reading — the reading check names its five test words (on, dog, pen, ant, hand) and the rubric scores how many of the five a pupil blends and reads. Extra game (pp.140–141): a **banana/apple circle race** — the class sits in a big circle; a walker goes round the outside tapping shoulders and saying "banana" each time; when the tap comes with "apple," the tapped pupil must repeat "apple" and race the walker around the circle for the empty seat; the slower one becomes the walker. Read answers (p.143): 1. sun, 2. dad, 3. ten. Look-and-say answers (p.145): 1. I like fish. 2. I like bread. 3. I don't like cake. 4. I like rice. 5. I don't like banana. 6. I don't like soup. Look-and-count hidden-fruit answers (p.145): six bananas, ten apples, eight mangoes. A **sound-counting group game** (p.142): groups count the sounds in a spoken word, write the word, and score — worked example on the fingers: cup = first c, middle u, final p; played over up, sun, ten, it, run, hot, duck (and ham, dog, get in the splitting drill). The **"Titanic" game** (p.144): the teacher calls a number 1–10 at random; pupils must form groups of exactly that many; anyone left without a group stops playing and sits down.

### Unit 6 Let's play

- **L21 Having fun (pp.146–152).** Action verbs run, jump, sing, fly, hop, swim. Track 6.1 heard order: 1. jump, 2. fly, 3. run, 4. swim, 5. hop, 6. sing. **Soutta Says** returns as the action-verb game (p.147). Sound **l** mini-text (Track 6.2, p.148): "Houmphanh gives his cat some milk. She likes milk! She drinks the milk and licks her lips." **Story script, Track 6.4 "Houmphanh and the bird," printed in full (p.151)**: it is Saturday; Houmphanh, Payia and their friends are going to play; they hop and sing; they run and jump; (sound of a bird — chirp, chirp, chirp) suddenly Houmphanh hears a noise; he sees a yellow and green bird on the grass; the bird cannot fly; Houmphanh and Payia help the bird until it can fly again; then they take the bird back to the rice field so the bird can fly home; the bird flaps its wings and flies away. True/False statements with answers (p.151): Houmphanh and Champa are playing = No (Houmphanh and Payia); in picture 2 the children hop and sing = Yes; in picture 3 the children run and swim = No (run and jump); the bird is blue and yellow = No (green and yellow); the bird can fly after they help it = Yes. — This settles the student-book filing's open point: the story is wordless **on the student book's printed page only**; its words exist, on this track.
- **L22 I can… / I can't… (pp.153–159).** Assessed: speaking. Grammar chant Tracks 6.5/6.6 printed in full (p.154): "I can jump, but I can't hop. I can sing, but I can't fly. I can run, but I can't swim." Sound **f** mini-text (Track 6.7, p.156): "Sounthone and his family are fishing at the river. Sounthone catches five fish. It is fun." Track 6.9 Listen and point dialogue script printed in full (p.158): a speaker asks six people "Can you…?" — Sounthone: run? Yes, I can. Mum: fly? No, I can't. Payia: jump? No, I can't. Houmphanh: sing? Yes, I can. Grandma: hop? No, I can't. Champa: swim? Yes, I can. Printed picture key in heard order: 3, 5, 6, 1, 2, 4.
- **L23 Toys (pp.160–165).** Toys car, doll, marble, bike, ball; assessed: writing. Opener is a guide-only game: **slow-reveal guessing** — a toy picture hidden behind paper is uncovered a little at a time while pupils guess (the same game opens L24). **Toy-box chant, Track 6.10, printed in full (p.161)**: "What's in the toy box? Is it a marble? No, it isn't! Is it a car? No, it isn't! Is it a ball? No, it isn't! Is it a bike? No, it isn't! Is it a doll? Yes, it is!" Sound **b** mini-text (Track 6.11, p.162): "The boys have a big blue ball. They are bouncing the ball with their hands." Order-the-pictures answer key for the bird story (p.165): story order = pictures **5, 2, 1, 4, 3**.
- **L24 Let's practice! (pp.166–170).** Creative lesson; assessed: reading — the check is matching rhyming words (the -un, -an, -ot families). Read-and-match printed answers (p.168): 1. dad, sad; 2. fun, run; 3. can, man; 4. pot, hot. Word-changing drills (pp.168–169): change the first letter — hat → cat, bat → fat; the -en family pen, ten, hen, men. Make-the-words answers (p.169): 1. fan, 2. leg, 3. bin (with first/middle/final sound questions worked for fan: f–a–n). Extra games: mime a sound's action while the class stays silent and one pupil guesses the sound (p.168); a Yes/No thumbs game over toy pictures ("Is it a ball?", p.170); a **can/can't sides-of-the-room game** (p.170) — right side of the room is "can," left side is "can't," pupils walk to their side for each "Can you swim?"; and a group board-tap game over toy pictures (Activity 6).

### Unit 7 My home

- **L25 My house (pp.171–177).** Home words sofa, table, bed, TV, door, window. Track 7.1 heard order: 1. TV, 2. door, 3. sofa, 4. table, 5. bed, 6. window. Sound **j** mini-text (Track 7.2, p.173): "Sounthone is in his garden. There is a big mango tree. Sounthone is jumping to pick a mango" — the guide confirms "There is a…" is seeded here, a unit before its grammar lesson. **Story script, Track 7.4 "Champa's new house," printed in full (pp.175–176)**: today Champa's family are moving to a new house, but Champa feels sad — "I like my house. I don't want to move." Mum: "Champa, it's time to go. Are you ready?" — "Okay, mum." She says goodbye to her bedroom ("Goodbye, bedroom"), goes to the garden and says goodbye to the mango tree, goes to the gate and says goodbye to the gecko ("Goodbye, gecko" — spelled in full here; the student book's own story page prints "Googbye"), and gets into the car; the family drives to the new house and Champa is still sad; Dad: "Come on Champa, let's look at your new bedroom" — "Wow! I like my new bedroom!" Champa is very happy. Extra activity (p.177): listen-and-mime — pupils mime sad (a sad face), goodbye (wave), happy (a happy smile) whenever the word is heard as the story plays again.
- **L26 There is a… (pp.178–184).** Assessed: speaking. Grammar chant Tracks 7.5/7.6 printed in full (p.179): "In my house, there is a TV / a sofa / a table / a window / a door / a bed." Sound **z** mini-text (Track 7.7, p.181): "Payia is in her garden picking flowers. There is a big bee zooming around the flowers. It starts to buzz. Zzzzzzz." Track 7.9 Listen and point script (p.183): There is a bag on the chair; a ball under the window; a car in the toy box; a book on the desk; a cat under the bed; a doll in the bed — printed picture key in heard order: 4, 6, 3, 1, 5, 2.
- **L27 What colour is it? (pp.185–190).** New colours pink, purple, orange, black, brown, taught off real coloured objects brought to class; assessed: writing (letters j, z, w). **The colour chant, Track 7.10 — printed in full (p.186)**: "Red and yellow and pink and green, / Orange and purple and blue, / Black and brown and white too!" — the ten-colour set in one chant. Classroom colour hunt (p.186): "Point to something red" — pupils find and point to something of the colour in the room. Sound **w** mini-text (Track 7.11, p.187): "Houmphanh is walking. Suddenly a storm starts. The rain falls and the wind blows. Houmphanh is very wet." Order-the-pictures answer key for Champa's new house (p.190): story order = pictures **3, 5, 2, 1, 6, 4**.
- **L28 Let's practice! (pp.191–196).** Creative lesson; rhyming families -in, -og, -en; assessed: reading. **Listen and draw** (pp.191–192): the teacher dictates a room — "Draw a yellow sofa. Draw a blue table. Draw a green book on the table…" — and pupils draw it; then each pupil draws their own dream room or dream house and describes it. Read-and-match printed answers (p.193): 1. cat, mat; 2. bin, win; 3. dog, jog; 4. pen, ten — with word-changing drills (can → fan; hot → pot, not, dot). Complete-the-words answers (p.194): 1. dad, 2. run, 3. bed, 4. sock. **Find the differences — both pictures described in full printed sentences (pp.195–196)**, eleven differences in all: picture a's sofa is yellow, b's is brown; a has two chairs, b three; a's TV is black, b's red; a has a green book on the table, b an orange book (and a purple book); a has one window, b two; a ball under a's table, a car under b's; a white doll on a's sofa, a pink doll on b's; a black bag on the chair with nothing in it, b a white bag with a book in it; a white dog under a's sofa, a black cat under b's.
- **L29 Revision (pp.197–201).** The whole-course review and its assessment lesson: the guide assesses by observation across **all three hours and every stage** (p.197), with one 0–3 rubric for how much of the Grade 3 language a pupil remembers. The day runs the Alphabet Song (Track 4.15), the Titanic game with numbers 1–10, Soutta Says over the year's word sets — the guide lists the review sets in full (p.198): classroom instructions (stand up, sit down, point to, look, listen, say, open your book), feelings (happy, sad, hot, cold, tired), animals (cat, dog, duck, pig, cow, chicken), actions (hop, jump, sing, run, swim, fly) — a colour hunt, and two guide-only games: a **letter relay** (p.199) — teams line up; a sound is whispered to the back pupil, each pupil writes the letter on the back of the pupil in front, and the front pupil writes the whole word on the board; first correct team scores — and a **sound hunt** (p.199) — the teacher says a sound, five-pupil teams race to find the picture whose word starts with it (worked examples: p–pen, s–sun, h–hat, a–ant, i–in). Point-and-say printed answers (p.198): 1. rice, 2. a red bike, 3. a pink sofa, 4. a TV, 5. a pink doll, 6. fish, 7. a purple table, 8. a banana, 9. a brown door, 10. a white marble. Write-the-word answers (p.200): 1. pen, 2. dad, 3. red. Closing game (pp.200–201): a **ball-throw counting game** — the class throws a ball round a circle counting to 10; whoever holds the ball at 10 answers a question, then asks the next one; a pupil who cannot answer is out; the guide prints the full review question set: What's your name? / What's this? / This is my brother / Is it a…? (Yes, it is. No, it isn't) / I am hot; He/She is tired / I like soup / I don't like fish / Do you like rice? (Yes, I do. No, I don't) / Can you jump? / I can swim / I can't fly / There is a (bed). **Track 8.1 — the book's last listening script, printed in full (p.201)**: eight mini-dialogues — 1. "What's your name?" "My name is Sounthone." 2. "I am happy." 3. "Can you jump?" 4. "What's this?" 5. "It's a pen." (Houmphanh) 6. "This is my mum." 7. "Do you like bread?" "No, I don't." 8. "Is this a car?" "Yes, it is." — plus "There is a cat on the sofa." Printed picture key (dialogue → picture): 1 → 8, 2 → 4, 3 → 6, 4 → 1, 5 → 7, 6 → 3, 7 → 2, 8 → 5.

## The phonics apparatus the guide adds (all 21 sounds)

The student book shows one picture set per sound; the guide gives every sound **a body action, a letter-formation rhyme, and its mini-text in print**. Actions (mime…): **s** — make a snake shape with your hand (p.14) · **a** — fingers walk up your arm like an ant (p.21) · **t** — hands swim like a turtle (p.29) · **i** — scratch an itch on your leg (p.42) · **p** — peel a papaya (p.50) · **n** — ride a motorbike (p.58) · **c/k** — hand to mouth, call someone (p.70) · **e** — break an egg (p.78) · **h** — fan your face as if hot (p.86) · **d** — play a drum (p.97) · **m** — rub your stomach as if eating something delicious (p.104) · **r** — growl like a dog and show your teeth (p.111) · **o** — turn a switch on and off (p.122) · **g** — a gecko climbing on a wall (p.129) · **u** — put up an umbrella (p.135) · **l** — a cat drinking milk (p.148) · **f** — hold a fishing rod and fish (p.156) · **b** — bounce a ball (p.162) · **j** — jump (p.173) · **z** — fly like a bee (p.181) · **w** — hold your hand flat in front of your mouth and blow (p.187).

Formation rhymes (the guide's stroke words, for the trace/copy pages): **s** "Go round, down and around" (p.15) · **a** "Go round, up, down and flick" (p.22) · **t** "Go down, round and lift your pencil then cross at the top" (p.29) · **i** "Go down and flick on the right hand side. Lift your pencil and make a dot on the head" (p.43) · **p** "Go down, up and round" (p.50) · **n** "Go down, up, around, down and flick" (p.58) · **curly c** "Go around" (p.71) · **kicking k** "Go down, up, up and across, back and down and across" (p.71) · **e** "Go across and around" (p.78) · **h** "Go down, go up, go around, down and flick" (p.86) · **d** "Go half round, up, down and flick" (p.97) · **m** "Go down, up, round, down, up, round and down" (p.105) · **r** "Go down, up and half round" (p.111) · **o** "Go around" (p.123) · **g** "Go around, up, down and around" (p.130) · **u** "Go down, round, up, down and flick" (p.136) · **l** "Go down and flick" (p.149) · **f** "Go around, down, lift your pencil and go across" (p.156) · **b** "Go down, up and all the way round" (p.163) · **j** "Go down, flick on the left hand side. Lift your pencil and make a dot on the top of the letter" (p.174) · **z** "Go across, down and across" (p.181) · **w** "Go down, up, down and up" (p.188). Tracing always starts "On the yellow dot," and letters are practised by finger-writing on the palm, the desk, and a friend's back before pencil work.

- The **hello song's words** (student book p.1, audio only) are printed in the guide, pp.8–9 — gap filled.
- The Introduction's **seven classroom-action words** (student book pp.2–3, pictures only) are printed in the guide, p.8, with the Track 0.2 script and key, p.11 — gap filled.
- The **feelings song's words** (student book L11 p.51, audio only) are printed in the guide, pp.83–84 — and the set is **five** feelings (happy, sad, hot, cold, tired), not six; the student book's sixth numbered picture is one of the pictured children in a feeling state, not a sixth feeling word — gap filled, set corrected.
- **Houmphanh and the bird** (student book L21) is wordless only on the student book's page; its full spoken text is Track 6.4, printed in the guide, p.151.
- The **p** lesson's word set: the guide's objectives (p.47) name pen, pan, pencil, pumpkin, pig; the student book's Say-the-sound page pictures pot, pencil, pig — both stand as printed in their own books.

## New for the course (from this guide)

- **A complete action for every phonics sound** (list above, with pages): 21 mime actions, one per sound — ready-made media slots for the Sounds Together level, in the platform's pictures-and-sound-first shape; the student book alone gives no actions.
- **A formation rhyme for every letter** (list above, with pages): the stroke words a teacher says while writing — the exact voice layer for the Pencil and Lines tracing lessons, including the two-shape split for one sound, curly c vs kicking k (p.71), and "start on the yellow dot" as the universal tracing rule.
- **Every Laos G3 song and chant in words** (the student book carries most of them in audio only): the hello song (pp.8–9), the five-colour chant (p.27), Five little pigs (pp.55–56), the happy song (pp.83–84), the Animal Song (p.109), the Alphabet Song (p.116), the ten-colour chant (p.186), and every grammar chant — new Laos evidence for Hello and Your Name, Numbers (the counting-down pigs song teaches the plural -s inside itself, p.55), the feelings set, and in/on/under.
- **The feelings set, settled**: five words — happy, sad, hot, cold, tired — each with its song action (pp.82–84).
- **An assessment design worth borrowing**: one assessed skill per lesson on a fixed rotation (vocabulary+listening → grammar+speaking → writing → reading), 0–3 rubrics converted to /10, and the class split into two groups that alternate assessed lessons so assessment fits a big class (pp.5–6); the Revision lesson assesses by observation across the whole day (p.197).
- **Games stock**: Soutta Says (pp.9–11), colour corners (p.28), What's missing? (p.39), the grouping game (p.56), the letter-tap team race (p.92), the Titanic grouping game (p.144), the banana/apple circle race (pp.140–141), the sound-counting group game (p.142), slow-reveal guessing (pp.160, 166), can/can't sides of the room (p.170), the letter relay (p.199), the sound hunt (p.199), and the ball-throw counting Q&A game (pp.200–201).
- **Story-ordering answer keys** for both re-used stories (bird story: 5, 2, 1, 4, 3 — p.165; new house story: 3, 5, 2, 1, 6, 4 — p.190): the check shape the Stories level can copy.
- **First/middle/final sound questions** as a named step (cup = c-u-p on the fingers, p.142; sun, fan worked the same way, pp.143, 169) — a Sounds Together step beyond first sounds.

**Slips recorded as printed (guide only):** in the Track 3.7 echo chant the lines print as "This my brother." and "This my sister." — the "is" dropped — while Track 3.6 and the lesson objectives print "This is my…" (p.77) · the Track 2.11 script's heard order (3. desk, 4. book) and the feedback key printed on the same page (3. book, 4. desk) disagree (p.52) · the L9 story's English text names the festival the "Krer festival" while its own Lao text names the Khmu New Year festival (pp.73–74) · the pig's sound "Oud!" and the dog's "Hong!" run through the guide's English chants, songs and stories as printed, where the other four animals carry the English sounds (Meow, Quack, Moo, Cluck) — Lao animal sounds inside the English material, as in the student book · several Listen-and-point keys are printed in picture order while their tracks play in a different order (e.g. L18 Track 5.9, p.132; L19 fruit counts, p.134; L29 Track 8.1, p.201) — noted so the keys are not misread as the heard order.

**FULLY READ 2026-10-27.** Read page by page from the text layer (the book has a real text layer; Lao and English extract together, the Lao in two-column flow). I'm great!" (pp.8–9) · Track 0.2 answer key, pictures 3, 4, 1, 6, 5, 7, 2 (p.11) · letter s formation "Go round, down and around" (p.15) · the dog barks "Hong!" in The dog's name (p.17) · colour chant "The snake is green" (p.27) · "Five little pigs" song (p.55) · the happy song "If you're happy and you know it" (p.83) · Track 3.16 "Sounthone feels happy" (p.93) · Track 4.9 answers "Yes, it is" (p.107) · Animal Song "The cat is on the roof" (p.109) · Alphabet Song "Now I know my abc" (p.116) · The birthday cakes "I have two cakes" (p.124) · the bird "a yellow and green bird" (p.151) · "Goodbye, gecko" (p.176) · colour chant "Black and brown and white too" (p.186) · Track 8.1 "There is a cat on the sofa" (p.201) · ISBN 978-9932-00320-4 (back page, printed p.202) · print run 13,000 copies (back page, printed p.202) — 20 anchors, all on their pages.

## Grade 4 (ປ.4) Student Textbook, 2012 — FULLY READ + CITATION CHECK PASSED 2026-10-27

The second book of the series ("STUDENT'S BOOK 2") — the **original
2012 Book 2**, not a revised edition: the filed Grade 3 book is the
2021 revision of Book 1, so this book continues the series the older
way (no phonics spine; see the standing-against-Grade-3 note inside).
Source copy: the public mirror the library's secured copy came from
(GitHub Xaypanya/Lao-Textbook, `textbooks/primary/p4/p4_english.pdf`).
Working copy verified against the library record before reading:
**7,766,358 bytes, 153 PDF pages — both match the record exactly
(VERIFIED-TEXT)**. Read by a checking agent; spot-verified before
commit (file facts, and the "Cleanthe board" slip on printed p.52).

## The book itself

"ENGLISH FOR LAO PRIMARY SCHOOLS — STUDENT'S BOOK 2 — GRADE 4," Ministry of Education and Sports, Research Institute for Educational Sciences (RIES), **2012**. The English Introduction (PDF p.4) dates and frames the series: this Grade 4 book is "the second in a series of three textbooks" (Grades 3–5) built for the reform of general education from 11 years to 12; under that reform English entered the primary curriculum from Grade 3, starting in academic year 2009–2010; the books were written by the Foreign Language Research and Resource Center at RIES with the Faculty of Letters, National University of Laos, and this book's development was financed by **AusAID and Save the Children Norway (SCN)**. The Introduction states the method in one line — contents "mainly focus on the illustration base learning," activities stressing the four skills (listening, speaking, reading, writing) to build basic knowledge for further study — and admits the book "is produced under the time pressure; therefore, some minor errors could not be avoided" (the slips list below bears that out). Credits (PDF p.2): Advisor Phouangkham Somsanith; Editor in Chief Assoc. Prof. Dr. Bouasavanh Keovilay; Managing Editors Khamphanh Phimsipasom and Viengkham Phonpraseuth; Writers Manivone Phaxayavong, Bouakeo Vilaylath, Thongsouk Keomany, Bounthapany Bounxouay; design and layout Khonesavanh Chanthavixay. PDF p.3 is a Lao preface; PDF p.5 is the acknowledgements ("ACKNOWLEGEMENTS" as printed). **No ISBN is printed anywhere in the book** — checked on the cover spread, the credits page and the back pages; the back cover is a blank pink panel with one short Lao printer line left empty. PDF p.1 is the whole cover spread (back cover, spine, front cover on one landscape sheet): the front cover shows two foxes, one asking "What's your favourite colour?" and the other answering "Green" beside a thought-bubble tree. Every interior page carries the footer "Ministry of Education and Sports — Research Institute for Educational Sciences," a printed folio, and a faint slanted Lao "RIES" watermark.

**Standing against the Grade 3 book filed before it.** The filed Grade 3 student book is the **2021 revised edition** of Book 1 of this same series; this Grade 4 is the **original 2012 Book 2**, written a decade before that revision. So Grade 4 continues the series the older way, and several Grade 3 (2021) features do **not** carry over: there is **no phonics spine at all** — no Say the sound, no Trace/Copy/Find the letter, no sound mini-texts; letter work shrinks to one capital↔lowercase matching page (p.3) and the alphabet shown as a song page (p.9). The six-panel picture stories are gone too. What replaces them is a fixed dialogue-lesson machine (below), photographs of real schoolchildren in red-scarf uniform carrying many speaking pages where Grade 3 used drawings, and a bilingual back matter (Vocabulary + Expressions, English–Lao) that Grade 3 does not have. The name stock overlaps only in Kham and Noy; the Grade 4 cast is Pha, Noy, Kham, Aloun, Sack, Naly, Boun, Keo, Vanh, Sone, Thong, Vieng, Nanthida (and Jenny in the birthday song).

**Structure.** One running head, "Every day English," over **18 lessons**: fourteen content lessons, three short Revision lessons (after Lessons 4, 9 and 14) and a final Revision 1–17. Every content lesson is two sections (A and B), and every section runs the same numbered activity shape: **1 Look, listen and repeat** (a speech-bubble dialogue) → **2 Look and say** (the vocabulary set or pattern over pictures/photos) → **3 Read and match** (sentences/words to pictures, or English to Lao) → **4 Listen and tick ✓** (a/b pairs, pictures or printed sentences) → **5 Look and write** (copy the model sentences, then gapped copies with missing letters) → in many sections **6 Let's write** (gapped words/sentences, word-order tiles, or arithmetic answered in words) → **Let's talk** (speech-bubble frame to speak) → some sections close with **Let's sing / Let's chant / Let's play**. The Contents (front section, printed pp.1–6) prints each lesson's target dialogues beside a **Competencies** table — what the child can do, with the lesson's page range; the ranges match the body's printed pages exactly (L1 pp.1–9 … L18 pp.135–139).

## The lesson map

- **Lesson 1 Names and Counting (pp.1–9).** **Section A: Names (pp.1–4).** Dialogue (p.1): "Hello. I'm Pha. / What's your name? — Hi. My name is Noy. / How are you, Noy? — I am fine. Thank you." — with the book's first contraction box printed under it: **"I am = I'm · What is = What's" (p.1)**. Look and say (p.2) over the name set Aloun, Sack, Naly, Boun. Read and match (p.3): the 26 capital letters matched to their lowercase forms, the book's only letter-formation page. Listen and tick (p.3); Look and write — copy "What's your name? / My name is Kham. / How are you?" (p.4); Let's talk (p.4). **Section B: Counting (11–20) (pp.5–9).** Dialogue (p.5): "What number is it? — It's ten. / Is it five? — Yes, it is. / Is it six? — No. It's eleven." Look and say (p.6, checked on the render): the words **eleven–twenty, each printed over its numeral**. Read and match words to numerals (p.7); Listen and tick (p.8); Look and write incl. the question "Is it twenty-two?" (p.8 — see slips); Let's talk over big numerals 11/15/13 (p.9); **Let's sing (p.9, checked on the render): the alphabet — all 26 capital letters printed in colour as the song's page; the song itself is audio.**
- **Lesson 2 Classroom objects (singular) (pp.10–18).** **Section A: What's this? (pp.10–14).** Dialogue (p.10): "What's this? — It's a book / It's a pencil." Look and say (p.11): desk, table, chair, chalk, board, bench. Read and match six "It's a…" sentences to pictures (p.12); Listen and tick (p.13); Look and write (p.13); Let's talk (p.14); **Let's sing (p.14): a "Hey! What's this?" song whose answers are missing-letter words — "It's a p_nc_l," "It's a b_g," "It's a crayon," "It's a p_ _," "It's a b_ _k," closing "Wow! It's red."** **Section B: Is this a…? (pp.15–18).** Dialogue (p.15): "Is this a book? — Yes, it is. / Is this a ruler? — No. It's a calculator." Look and say (p.16): classroom, map, pencil case, sharpener, bag, calculator. Read and match (p.17) adds eraser ("It's an eraser"); Listen and tick (p.17); Look and write (p.18); Let's talk (p.18).
- **Lesson 3 Classroom objects (plural) (pp.19–27).** **Section A: What are these? (pp.19–22).** Dialogue (p.19): "What are these? — They are pens. / Are these pencils? — Yes, they are. / Are these pencils? — No. They are books." Look and say (p.20): desks, tables, chairs, chalks, boards, sharpeners — and the book's second grammar box printed on the page: **"This is a pen. / These are pens. · It / They" (p.20)**. Read and match (p.21); Listen and tick (p.21); Look and write with missing-letter plurals ("These are c_a_r_" = chairs, p.22); Let's talk (p.22). **Section B: How many…? (pp.23–27).** Dialogue (p.23): "How many pens? — Two pens. / How many erasers? — Five erasers." Look and say (p.24): the number words **twenty-one to thirty** printed in full. Read and match words to numerals, twenty–thirty (p.25). Listen and tick with printed options (p.26: "Twenty chairs / Ten chairs," "Twenty-five students / Thirty students," "Eleven tables / Twenty-two tables," "Twenty-three books / Twenty-nine books"). **Look and write (p.26): arithmetic answered in English words — worked example "12+5= 17 seventeen," then 18+7 (twenty-five, gapped), 30−7, 8×3, 24:3 with the word to write.** Let's talk (p.27); **Let's sing (p.27): a counting song on the "One little, two little, three little…" frame, counting sneakers up 1→10 ("Ten little purple sneakers, / How many sneakers? / Ten sneakers!") and back down 10→1.**
- **Lesson 4 Friends and Family (pp.28–36).** **Section A: Friends (pp.28–31).** Dialogue (p.28): "Mom, I am home. This is my friend, Vanh. — Nice to meet you. — Nice to meet you, too." Look and say (p.29) with Sone, Keo, Naly, Thong. Read and match — five English sentences matched to their Lao translations (p.30). Listen and tick with printed sentence pairs (p.30). Look and write (p.31); Let's talk (p.31). **Section B: Family (pp.32–36).** Dialogues (p.32): "Keo, this is my mother. — It's nice to meet you. — It's nice to meet you, too, Keo." and the same with father for Sone. Look and say (p.33): father, mother, brother, sister. Read and match English→Lao (p.34); Listen and tick (p.34); Look and write with missing letters ("This is my m_th__," p.35); **Let's write (p.35): five gapped dialogue sentences to complete** ("Hi, Mom! I'm …………." / "This is my …………., Kham." / "It's ………. to ……….. you."); Let's talk (p.36); **Let's sing (p.36): The family song — "This is my mother / father / sister / brother" verses, each answered "Nice to meet you. / Nice to meet you, too."**
- **Lesson 5 Revision (Lessons 1–4) (pp.37–39).** Look, listen and repeat (p.37): four numbered mini-dialogues recapping numbers ("It is twelve / No. It is eighteen" — full forms, no contractions), What's this?, What are these? + How many?, and the friend introduction. Read and match English→Lao, seven items (p.38). Listen and tick (p.38) — its options include two patterns not taught yet: "How old are you?" (Lesson 9's) as a distractor, and "There are twenty chairs / There are eleven chairs" — **"There are" appears only here in the whole book**. Look and write (p.39); Let's write missing letters incl. "It's a pencil c_s_" and "Hi, my n__e __ kham" (p.39).
- **Lesson 6 Family members (pp.40–48).** **Section A: Parents (pp.40–43).** Dialogue (p.40): "Who's she? — She's my mother. / Who's he? — He's my father." Look and say (p.41): grand father, grand mother, brother, sister, friend, mother, father — with the contraction box **"Who is = Who's · She is = She's · He is = He's" (p.41)**. Read and match English→Lao (p.42); Listen and tick (p.42); Look and write (p.43); Let's talk (p.43). **Section B: Family member (pp.44–48).** Dialogue (p.44): "Who's he? — He's my father. He's tall. / Who's she? — She's my mother. She's short." Look and say (p.45): describing the family — grandfather/old, brother/young, mother/short, father/tall — with the opposites printed with the not-equal sign: **"old ≠ young · tall ≠ short" (p.45)**. Read and match four "My father is tall"-type sentences to pictures A–D (p.46); Listen and tick (p.46); Look and write (p.47); Let's write missing letters ("My father is t_ll," p.47); Let's talk over a six-person family table (p.48); **Let's sing (p.48): a "Who's she? / Who's he?" song with the answers left blank to fill from the family words, closing on the word "Family."**
- **Lesson 7 Commands (pp.49–57).** **Section A: Positive commands (pp.49–53).** Dialogue (p.49): "Clean the board, please. — Thank you. — You're welcome." Look and say (p.50): Sweep the floor, Close the door, Clean the board, Open the window (each with and without "please"). Read and match to pictures (p.51); Listen and tick (p.51); Look and write (p.52 — see slips for "Cleanthe board"); Let's write — complete the verb ("_ _ _ _ the window, please," p.52); Let's talk (p.53); **Let's chant (p.53): the four commands as a chant, "Open the window, please. (2 times) / Close the door, please. / Clean the board, please. / Sweep the floor, please."** **Section B: Negative commands (pp.54–57).** Dialogue (p.54): "Don't sleep! — I'm sorry. / Don't talk! — We're sorry." — contraction box **"do not = don't · we are = we're" (p.54)**. Look and say (p.55): eat, sleep, copy, talk (Don't eat! etc.). Listen and tick with printed pairs (p.56); Read and match English→Lao (p.56) adds Don't drink!; Look and write — copy each Don't-sentence three times (p.57); Let's talk (p.57).
- **Lesson 8 School Activities (pp.58–65).** **Section A: Let's do it (pp.58–61).** Dialogue (p.58): "Let's clean the desk. — Yes. Let's do it. / Let's erase the board. — Yes. Let's do it." Look and say (p.59): Let's listen to the teacher, Let's read a story, Let's write in the book, Let's look at the board. Read and match English→Lao (p.60); Listen and tick with printed pairs (p.60); **Look and write (p.61, checked on the render): after the copying, the book's first word-order exercise — word tiles to rebuild: "clean / Let's / table / the" → "Let's clean the table." (worked), then "the / board / Let's / erase" and "teacher / listen to / the / Let's."** Let's talk (p.61). **Section B: Can you…? (pp.62–65).** Dialogue (p.62): "Can you sweep the floor? — Yes, of course. / Can you open the window? — I'm sorry. I'm busy." — Can you…? as a **request for help**, not ability. Look and say (p.63): Can you water the garden? / feed the chickens? / clean the yard? Read and match phrases to pictures (p.64); Listen and tick (p.64); Look and write (p.65); Let's talk (p.65).
- **Lesson 9 Age and Counting (30–50) (pp.66–74).** **Section A: Age (pp.66–69).** Dialogue (p.66): "Happy birthday, Nanthida! How old are you? — I'm sixteen years old." Look and say (p.67): "I'm 9 years old" and ages 8–15 in numerals. Read and match English→Lao (p.68: eight, fifteen, eleven, twelve in words); Listen and tick (p.68); Look and write (p.69); Let's talk (p.69); **Let's sing (p.69): a birthday song — "It's my birthday today / It's your birthday today… Happy birthday, Jenny! / One, two, three, four, five, six, / Seven years old! / Now I'm seven years old. / Now you're seven years old."** **Section B: Counting (pp.70–74).** Dialogue (p.70): "What number is it? — It's thirty. / Is it thirty? — No. It's thirty-one." Look and say (p.71, checked on the render): **every number 31–50 printed as numeral over word (thirty-one … forty … fifty)**. Listen and tick (p.73); **Look and write (p.73): arithmetic in words again — "31 + 5= 36 thirty-six" worked, then 16×2 (thirty-two, gapped), 50−7 (forty-three, gapped), 22+13, 80:2, 30+7 to write in full.** Let's talk (p.74); **Let's play (p.74): a word-search grid — "Look for: ten, eight, twenty, nine, forty one, fifty."**
- **Lesson 10 Revision (Lessons 6–9) (pp.75–77).** Listen and repeat (p.75): four dialogues — Who's she/he?, the board-cleaning exchange with Thank you / You're welcome and Let's clean the desk, "Don't sleep! — I'm sorry. I don't do it." (as printed) with Can you sweep the floor?, and the number/birthday exchange. Read and match English→Lao, eight items incl. Water the garden / Feed the chickens (p.76). Listen and tick (p.76). Look and write (p.77); Let's write (p.77) mixing missing letters with two arithmetic-in-words items repeated from p.73.

(pp.91–95).** Dialogue (p.91): "Where are the books? — They're on the table. / Where are the pencils? — They're under the book." — contraction box **"They are = They're" (p.91)**. No, I can't. … Can you swim? / No, I can't. I can't swim. I can sing. Yes, I can. … Can you sing? / Yes, I can. I can sing." — first person Can you…? (Grade 3's ability pattern) returns inside the song.**

(pp.131–134).** Dialogue (p.131): "Can the bird fly? — Yes, it can. / Can the dog fly? — No, it can't." Look and say (p.132): The frogs can hop / The birds can fly / The crocodiles can crawl / The lions can run / The dogs can bark / The fish can swim. Circle the odd one out (p.137): six sets — father/mother/you; ten/six/nice; red/car/blue; book/pencil/tree; sunny/fly/cold; cat/table/frog** ("sunny" appears here only; see slips). **Let's play Bingo (p.138): two number cards running to 50.** Answer the questions (p.138: Do you want…? — Yes, I do / No. I don't). Let's talk (p.139): How old / weather / favourite-colour frames.
